# Engineering's rulings, for your veto

**What this is.** Every ruling engineering has made on the class's model, and on how the model was made, that is in force, written so you can veto it without reading code. Each says:
- what it decides;
- why;
- what it risks if it is wrong;
- what the alternative would be.

You asked for this on 31 October. A ruling's code (M-6, S2-R1, `the ruling after SH-43`) follows its plain words only as its handle in the files.

**Its state.** Drafted on 31 October 2026 from the repository at `27ba96d2`, by two agents on the brief [`V-veto.md`](../studies/class-model/briefs/V-veto.md), then joined and checked by me. It comes to you at GM. Before then:
- the rulings of the held pass's second stage, the omission review and the final review are added;
- a ruling the final review corrects is rewritten.

The architecture plan's own decisions are listed with the plan. Your own decisions are in `founder-decisions.tsv`, not here.

**How to veto.** Name a ruling by its title. A vetoed ruling stops. Engineering then takes its alternative, or the one you name, amends the model where the ruling placed lines, and tells you what moved. A ruling you don't veto stands.

**How it is laid out:**
- First, the rulings that could move the most, one line each, linked to their entries.
- Then every entry: first those made before the held pass, then those from it on. Each comes in six groups:
  1. who does a thing;
  2. what is one capability;
  3. scope and conditions;
  4. what is no thing of the class;
  5. what the model reads;
  6. how the work is run.
- Rulings of one kind that are many (Q1's unclear pairs, the subjects' points, the new things, the Shopify digests' rules) are tables, with their shared risk and alternative said once.

## The words

- **built in** (`core`): the platform does it itself, built into it.
- **its own service** (`own-service`): a service of the platform's own company, apart from its core.
- **left to apps** (`extension-point`): left to apps or partners, through a place the platform makes for them. The entries before the held pass say *apps*.
- **outside** (`outside`): done outside the platform, by a third party.
- **shown only** (`shows`): the evidence only shows the thing exists, and places it nowhere. The entries before the held pass say *shows*.
- **no thing of the class** (`out`): not a thing a platform of this class does for its stores, such as a screen's frame or a tool of the company apart from any store.
- **nothing held** (`none-held`): the evidence read holds nothing for that cell, found by the terms searched. It never means the platform lacks the thing.
- **a cell:** one platform's entry for one capability.
- **a capability's count and kind:** the count is how many of the five platforms build it in or offer it as their own service. The kind is:
  - `class` where that count is at least one;
  - `slot` where every platform that shows it leaves it to apps, or says another party does it;
  - `open` otherwise (`method/class_kinds.py`).
- **Q1, a concern, an unclear pair:** Q1 is the concern map, the index of what each platform states, and a concern is one of its entries. An unclear pair is two concerns Q1 couldn't tell were one thing.
- **the held pass:** the reading, entry by entry, of every entry of the platforms' own lists that the model hadn't read, each placed on the model (30 and 31 October). The lists include routes, settings, navigation, plans, permissions, catalogs and changelogs.
- **a line:** one row of the held pass's placements, one entry placed on one capability. The held pass wrote 24,704 lines.
- **a maker statement:** held words that name a company as the maker or provider of a thing.

## How much each could move

- **High:** if it were wrong, a capability's kind or count would move, or many cells' support would. From the held pass on, a ruling that 100 or more lines name is high too.
- **Medium:** cells' support or scope would move, but no kind or count.
- **Low:** only wording, or the work's cost, would move.

Each weight is a reading. Each count behind it is a fact, counted by script from the files at the commit.

Before the held pass there are 41 rulings, 13 of them high, and two tables:
- Q1's 84 unclear pairs, 51 of which would move a count if ruled the other way;
- the subjects' points' 50 settled conflicts, 23 of them high.

From the held pass on there are 85 rulings, 21 of them high, and 270 table rows, 21 of them high.

## The rulings that could move the most

Each links to its entry. Weights are readings; the counts in them are facts from the files.

## Before the held pass

**Where you saw them first.** The orientation page, version 9 (30 October), listed 21 rulings under the heading *Engineering's rulings since the last report, for your veto*. Each ruling below that the page listed says so, with the page's item number. The page lives outside the repository, so each ruling is cited from its source here. The page's item 8, every amount referring to a currency record, is the architecture plan's decision, and the coordinator lists it with the plan's other decisions. It isn't repeated here.

### 1. Who does it, and who makes it

#### A platform's own interface for its own feature counts as built in
- **Decides:** a platform's own programming interface for its own feature counts as built in. That means its folder, object, access scope, webhook topic or a mutation's requirement, and it holds even in a guide written for app developers. Where the interface's own statements say apps or partners do the work, it counts as apps. Listed first on the orientation page, item 3.
- **Why:** the extent's brief counts the platform's own interface for its own feature as built in. The dry run used two methods: the anchor read Shopify's interface-only rows as shows, while WooCommerce's part read its own as built in. The review settled on the brief's test, and the 55 rows were placed again ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L16](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L16); [method-report.md L46](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L46)). It replaced the anchor's reading.
- **Risks:** in the dry run alone it moved 55 rows and three counts ([method-report.md L53](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L53)). Suppose an interface can exist without a merchant being able to use the feature. Then every cell whose built-in rests on interface rows alone would drop to shows, and its capability's count would fall by one. How many cells rest on such rows now isn't counted here, because the evidence rows don't mark which statements are interfaces. The part checker flags each one for the reviewer ([method/README.md L42](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/method/README.md?plain=1#L42)).
- **Instead:** count an interface as built in only where an admin page, a plan line or a help page also shows the feature, and read interface-only rows as shows. Counts would fall most on Shopify and WooCommerce, whose evidence leans on their developer documentation (a reading).
- **Weight:** high. It moved three counts in the dry run, and it applies to every platform.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L140](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L140)

#### A platform's own service counts only where a statement names its company
- **Decides:** a cell is the platform's own service only where a statement names the platform's company, or a company of its group, as the maker or provider. A brand in a product's name doesn't name its maker, and a note's inference never counts. Without such a statement, the cell is placed by what its statements show, and the missing statement is noted as a gap. Listed first on the orientation page, item 2.
- **Why:** the inspection of the review brief found that a brand-named product was being read as the platform's own service. The dry run's review then made maker evidence a statement that names the company ([method-report.md L21](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L21); [review-dry-run-rulings.md L20](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L20)).
- **Risks:** 1,640 evidence rows in 135 cells support own service. In 51 of those cells, own service is the only support that counts, and 7 capabilities' kind rests on them: CM-ACC-010, CM-TRU-019, CM-SEL-003, CM-CHK-005, CM-SHP-012, CM-MKT-004 and CM-SUP-003. If the test is too loose, those 7 would be open or slot, not class. If it is too strict, services that carry only the brand are placed as shows and left out of the counts. The anchor left Shopify Collective's maker and Shopify Combined Listings' maker as gaps on that ground ([B-anchor/notes.md L221](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/B-anchor/notes.md?plain=1#L221)).
- **Instead:** count a product that carries the platform's brand as its own service. Counts would rise wherever such a product is a cell's only evidence. How many cells that is isn't counted here.
- **Weight:** high. Seven kinds rest on own service alone.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L115](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L115)

#### One statement naming a service's provider counts for each feature of that service
- **Decides:** a statement that names the platform's company as a service's provider supports own service in each cell that shows a different feature of that service, as WooPayments' statement does. Where two domains count it, the merge flags it, and the final review weighs it as maker evidence, not as a feature counted twice. Listed first on the orientation page, item 13.
- **Why:** each cell shows its feature by the service's own statements. The maker statement only says who provides them ([E-woocommerce/notes.md L327](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/E-woocommerce/notes.md?plain=1#L327); [A-woocommerce/notes.md L325](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/A-woocommerce/notes.md?plain=1#L325)).
- **Risks:** WooPayments' provider statement, WC-S-0476, supports own service in four cells. Two of them count on it alone: WooCommerce's cells in CM-MKS-006 and CM-MKS-007. If one maker statement may count in only one cell, both of those capabilities fall from 5 to 4.
- **Instead:** let a maker statement count in one capability only, the one it names, and read it as shows elsewhere.
- **Weight:** high. Two counts rest on it.
- **Source:** [E-woocommerce/notes.md L327](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/E-woocommerce/notes.md?plain=1#L327)

#### WooCommerce is placed by its own statements, not by what WordPress does
- **Decides:** WooCommerce's cells are placed by WooCommerce's own statements. Where a statement shows WordPress doing the thing, the cell reads shows, and the reading names WordPress. This covers users and the Administrator role, multisite, themes and their editing, pages, menus and domains. WooCommerce's own roles and capabilities still count as built in. Each such cell is a reading, since no statement held says what WordPress does. Listed first on the orientation page, item 14.
- **Why:** the extent counts what each platform's own statements show. WordPress is another product, and the concern map holds no statement of it ([A-woocommerce/notes.md L45](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/A-woocommerce/notes.md?plain=1#L45); [E-woocommerce/notes.md L48](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/E-woocommerce/notes.md?plain=1#L48)).
- **Risks:** WooCommerce's cells are not-stated in CM-ACC-001 (merchant account and store owner, count 3), CM-STF-001 (staff accounts, count 4) and CM-ACC-003 (several stores under one account, count 2), where the statements show WordPress's users, roles and multisite. If what WordPress does counted as WooCommerce's, those three counts would each rise by one (a reading).
- **Instead:** treat WordPress as part of WooCommerce's core, since every WooCommerce store runs on it.
- **Weight:** high. Three counts would move.
- **Source:** [A-woocommerce/notes.md L45](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/A-woocommerce/notes.md?plain=1#L45)

#### A feature flag that isn't released only shows the feature
- **Decides:** a feature flag marked as not released is shows: it tells you the platform names the feature, not that a merchant has it. A released flag supports built in, scoped to its plan or store. One flag alone never makes a capability. Listed first on the orientation page, item 3.
- **Why:** an unreleased flag is the platform's own code naming a feature to come. A released flag is the feature switched on where its scope says ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L21](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L21)).
- **Risks:** if unreleased flags meant the feature is offered to some stores, the cells resting on them would count. If released flags didn't mean it's offered, the cells resting on them would be overstated, though scoped. The evidence rows don't mark flags apart, so neither number is counted here.
- **Instead:** read any flag as built in, scoped to the stores that hold it. A count would rise wherever an unreleased flag is a cell's only support.
- **Weight:** medium. It moves cells' support and scope. A count moves only where a flag is a cell's only support, which isn't counted here.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L141](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L141)

#### Services placed by who the statements name as their maker
- **Decides:** three services, by their maker evidence. Zid Pay is Zid's own service, since its terms are a contract with Zid's company. BigCommerce Payments is built in, since no statement held names BigCommerce's company as its maker. WooCommerce Shipping is Automattic's own service, by its terms. Listed first on the orientation page, item 18.
- **Why:** the own-service test above, applied to each service's own statements ([method-report.md L199](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L199)).
- **Risks:** built in and own service count alike, so choosing between them moves no count. It moves what architecture reads as the maker: a service the platform runs apart from its core, or a part of the core. A count would move only if a service were read as outside, made by a third party a statement names; whether any statement does is not checked here.
- **Instead:** read BigCommerce Payments as BigCommerce's own service, like Zid Pay, on the strength of the brand. That breaks the test above.
- **Weight:** medium. No kind or count moves between the two placements.
- **Source:** [method-report.md L199](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L199)

#### The staff permission, not the partner's, decides built in
- **Decides:** where Salla lists one permission for staff and for partners, the staff permission is cited for built in. A partner's permission shows an interface for apps. Where it decides a placement, the staff permission is cited.
- **Why:** the staff permission is the merchant's own team using the platform's feature. The partner's permission is what an app is allowed to reach ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L29](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L29); [review-dry-run-rulings.md L22](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L22)). It settled a dry-run count that rested on a partner's permission ([method-report.md L56](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L56)).
- **Risks:** a cell built only on a partner's permission would read apps, not built in. In the dry run this was one count.
- **Instead:** read either permission as built in, since both name the platform's feature.
- **Weight:** medium. It moves Salla cells' support. In the dry run it moved one count, and none are counted for it since.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L142](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L142)

#### An admin page named for an app counts as the app's
- **Decides:** an admin route named for an application, and gated only by the apps permission, is the app's page, so it counts as apps, not built in.
- **Why:** the route exists for an app to fill, so the platform only makes the place ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L19](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L19)).
- **Risks:** if such a route were the platform's own feature, the cells resting on it would count as built in, and their counts would rise. They aren't counted apart here.
- **Instead:** read any route in the platform's own route list as built in.
- **Weight:** medium. It moves cells' support. A count moves only where such a route is a cell's only support.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L143](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L143)

#### The subjects' points, settled one conflict at a time

S-01's and S-02's variation points were placed on the model, and each conflict was settled by engineering, from the evidence. There were 50 outcomes on 37 points: 43 amendments to the model, 4 dismissed and 3 kept open, each with what would settle it ([method-report.md L219](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L219)). None went to you, since no conflict questioned the evidence under one of your decisions. In almost every case the subject held what the concern map didn't: observed runs, the WooCommerce release's code, and help pages Q1 never placed ([method-report.md L220](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L220)). By the method report, 21 capabilities changed kind or count ([method-report.md L224](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L224)).

**What is shared:**
- **The risk of an amendment:** if the subject's statement doesn't say what the outcome reads, the cell goes back to what it held before. An amendment that raised a count would lower it again.
- **The alternative:** leave the model as the concern map had it, and keep each conflict as a question for the final review.
- **Weight:** high where the outcome raised a count or changed a kind (21 outcomes), or where a point dismissed or kept open would move one if it went the other way (2). Medium where the outcome changed a cell's support only (15), or where a point dismissed or kept open would move a cell (3). Seven outcomes repeat another point's amendment and take its weight. Low for 2 dismissals that would change nothing countable.
- **The table:** one row per outcome, from the points' files, with the point and the capability it moved. A row's own risk is given in its weight column. The point's link is its row.

### 2. What is one capability

#### The model is built domain by domain, from two anchors
- **Decides:** in each domain, Shopify and Salla, the two platforms with the most statements, are read together first, and the structure follows their own groupings. WooCommerce, Zid and BigCommerce are then mapped one at a time onto what is there. A platform that shows something the model lacks adds a capability. After each platform that adds one, a back-fill writes the new capability's cells for the platforms mapped before it, so the order doesn't bias the counts. Listed first on the orientation page, item 1.
- **Why:** this is domain analysis from representative systems: build from the richest, then check and extend against the others ([B-extent.md L5](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L5)).
- **Risks:** every capability is cut where Shopify's and Salla's groupings cut. A thing the later platforms group differently is mapped onto the anchors' cut. A landing that doesn't fit cleanly is marked strained, and so is one whose statements are cited in another tree: 219 of the 652 landings are (`merged/landings.tsv`). If the anchors' cut is wrong, capabilities would split or merge, and their counts would move. The merge leaves 1,038 one-capability questions for the final review (`merged/flags.tsv`).
- **Instead:** build each platform's model on its own, then merge the five; or anchor on all five at once. Either costs more, and might cut capabilities elsewhere.
- **Weight:** high. Every capability's cut rests on it.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L10](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L10)

#### A place of its own makes a capability, a plan line included
- **Decides:** something becomes a capability where at least one platform gives it a place of its own: an admin section or route, an interface group, a plan line or an app-store category. A single feature flag isn't enough. So BigCommerce's Persistent cart, a plan line, became a capability under the cart, and Zid's names in its inventory (a plan line, an interface group) were admitted as capabilities. What each kind of place is was defined exactly on 30 October ([B-extent.md L132](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L132)). Listed first on the orientation page, item 17.
- **Why:** a place the platform makes for a thing is the platform saying it is one thing. A flag is code, not a place ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L40](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L40); [method-report.md L198](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L198)).
- **Risks:** 78 capabilities count one platform (`merged/kinds.tsv`). Where one place is a capability's only ground, a stricter test would fold it into its parent, and the count of a capability of one would go. Three of Zid's four first new capabilities rest on names alone, and the final review checks whether each should fold ([method-report.md L98](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L98)).
- **Instead:** require a statement that describes the thing, or a place on two platforms. Fewer capabilities, and those of one platform would mostly fold into their parents.
- **Weight:** high. Capabilities exist or not by it.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L131](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L131)

#### One thing, one domain
- **Decides:** before a part adds a capability, it searches every domain's capabilities for the same thing, in both languages. Where another domain holds it, the concern moves there and the part adds nothing. The coordinator removed one capability made twice: the merchant's mobile app, made in domain A and already held in G. BigCommerce's Native Hosting moved to hosting of the store, in G ([G-amend/notes.md L5](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/G-amend/notes.md?plain=1#L5)), and Zid's bundle offers moved to bundles, in B ([B-amend/notes.md L5](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/B-amend/notes.md?plain=1#L5)). In selling and order management, WooCommerce's address autocomplete and Zid's voice agent for abandoned carts moved to customers and growth, and Zid's deduction from Zid Pay into its wallet moved to merchant and platform. Listed first on the orientation page, items 10 and 16.
- **Why:** each part read only its own domain's model, so nothing showed it the other's copy. A search at the point of adding costs less than a correction after the cells are built ([method-report.md L150](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L150); [method-report.md L197](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L197)).
- **Risks:** a thing made twice is counted twice, and a thing moved to the wrong domain is cut by that domain's structure. The search reads names, notes and terms, so two capabilities that use different words for one thing can still both stand. The merge's 1,038 one-capability questions are where the final review looks for them.
- **Instead:** let each domain keep what it found, and merge the duplicates at the end. Cheaper per part, but counts would stand twice until then.
- **Weight:** high. A duplicate doubles a count.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L67](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L67)

#### A statement counts once, where its words belong
- **Decides:** the concern map holds one statement under several concerns, each time with its own key, so the merge now finds a statement by its id. Where one cell rests on it alone, it counts in the capability its words are about, and shows elsewhere. So Shopify Collective, Shopify's own way to sell and source other stores' products, counts in its own capability, CM-CHN-005, and product sourcing, CM-CAT-010, counts Shopify through apps. Listed first on the orientation page, item 9.
- **Why:** a test that reads a key can't see what the key's statement is shared with: 548 of Shopify's statement ids, 124 of Salla's and 145 of WooCommerce's sit under several concerns ([method-report.md L140](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L140); [B-anchor/notes.md L318](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/B-anchor/notes.md?plain=1#L318)).
- **Risks:** of 25 statements counted in two domains at domain D, three were a cell's sole support. Each was corrected to count once, and the other 22 changed no count ([method-report.md L144](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L144)). If a statement may count wherever it is cited, product sourcing's Shopify cell would be built in again. If it may count only once even when it names several things, see the next ruling.
- **Instead:** count a statement under every concern Q1 put it in, as Q1 did.
- **Weight:** high. Counts moved by it, Shopify Collective's among them.
- **Source:** [method-report.md L144](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L144)

#### A statement that names several things counts in each
- **Decides:** a statement that names several things counts in each capability it names: a help section titled for two subjects, a fee on a shipping label, a pair of access scopes, a list. Each landing that holds one of them stays clean. This is the exception to the ruling above, which applies to a statement about one thing. Listed first on the orientation page, item 15.
- **Why:** the statement is evidence for each thing it names, by its own words ([method-report.md L195](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L195)). At the merge of every domain, the coordinator read the five statements counted twice where a cell rests on one alone. Each named several things or was a provider's statement ([method-report.md L193](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L193)).
- **Risks:** at that merge, five cells rested on such a statement alone. If a list or a two-subject title counted only once, those five would drop, and their capabilities' counts with them. The merge now flags 2,324 statements counted in two domains, most added by the held pass (a reading), which the final review weighs.
- **Instead:** count a statement that names several things once, in the capability its title names first, and read it as shows elsewhere.
- **Weight:** high. Five counts rested on it at the merge.
- **Source:** [method-report.md L195](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L195)

#### Each of Q1's unclear pairs is ruled joined, nested or apart, one pair at a time
- **Decides:** each pair of concerns in one of Q1's unclear candidates gets one ruling row, on keys from those two concerns only. A pair is joined where one capability's cells cite both sides, nested where a capability cites one side and its parent the other, and apart where no capability cites both. Where the other side's concern sits in another domain that names this one, a capability here may cite it, and the ruling follows the cells. Otherwise the pair is apart, with the domain `scope.tsv` gives the other side as its basis. A ruling follows the cells: no statement is left uncited to keep a pair apart. Listed first on the orientation page, item 5.
- **Why:** the dry run's review found rulings that contradicted the brief and rulings mixing pairs ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L25](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L25); [review-dry-run-rulings.md L26](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L26)). This replaced the checker's first test, which read any capability under a common parent as together, so apart was impossible for a coarse concern. Four rulings were corrected ([method-report.md L45](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L45)).
- **Risks:** 397 ruling rows over 84 candidates: 218 joined, 155 apart and 24 nested. 82 of the apart rows give `scope.tsv`'s domain for the other side as their basis, or part of it. So if the scope part routed a concern wrongly, some of those pairs would be joined. The table below gives each candidate's own risk. By its rule, 51 of the 84 would move a count if the other answer held (a reading).
- **Instead:** rule each candidate once, on what the two things are, whatever domain holds each side, and make the cells follow the ruling.
- **Weight:** high. Joins decide which platforms a capability counts.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L73](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L73)

#### Where two domains rule a pair differently, both stand until the final review
- **Decides:** each domain rules a pair from its own cells. Where two domains' cells give different answers, both rulings stand, the merge flags the pair, and the final review gives one answer. Listed first on the orientation page, item 11.
- **Why:** a part's checker sees only its own domain's cells, so each ruling is true of the cells it was made on ([E-anchor/notes.md L535](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/E-anchor/notes.md?plain=1#L535)).
- **Risks:** 18 pairs over 13 candidates hold two answers ([method-report.md L193](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L193); `merged/flags.tsv`). Kinds are computed from cells, not from rulings, so no count moves on the rulings themselves. A reading: in most of the 18, one domain has a capability that cites both sides and the other domain has none, so the two answers differ on where the pair is held, not on what it is. Four turn on the thing itself: U-051 (theme sales), U-056 (Shopify Collabs against Salla's partner programmes), U-074 (in-person payment hardware) and U-076 (Managed Markets against Salla's global selling programme).
- **Instead:** settle each difference at the merge, before the review. The coordinator would then rule alone.
- **Weight:** medium. No kind or count moves until the review answers.
- **Source:** [E-anchor/notes.md L535](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/E-anchor/notes.md?plain=1#L535)

#### Q1's unclear pairs, one row per candidate

The 397 rulings, grouped by candidate, built by script from `parts/*/rulings.tsv`, with each capability's count from `merged/kinds.tsv`.
- **Ruled, by part:** each part's ruling and the capability it rules onto, with ×n where a part ruled n pairs alike.
- **Basis:** the parts' rows in plain words, a reading of their bases.
- **If the other answer held:** computed from the merged files.
  - For a join: apart would take the pair's rows out of the capability. Its count falls only where a platform's place there rests on that concern's rows alone.
  - For apart: joined would make the two capabilities that hold the sides into one, counting the platforms either counts.
  - For nested: joined would fold the capability into its parent.
  - This is a reading. A real correction might move single statements, not whole capabilities.
- **Weight:** high where the other answer would move a count by that computation, medium otherwise.
- **The shared risk:** a join that is wrong counts a platform for a thing it doesn't have, and an apart that is wrong splits one thing into two capabilities, each counting fewer platforms.
- **The shared alternative:** the other answer, as the table gives it.
- **Source:** each row links to its first ruling row.

| Q1's candidate | Ruled, by part | Basis | If the other answer held | Weight | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

WooCommerce's order-recovery extensions join the recovery capability instead, under the rule for another domain's concern. | Apart would move this pair's rows out of CM-CHK-007, whose count of 4 holds on other rows; apart would move this pair's rows out of CM-MKT-022, whose count of 4 holds on other rows. | medium | [C-anchor/rulings.tsv L2](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/C-anchor/rulings.tsv?plain=1#L2) |
