#### A match whose other side lands later is written by the later part
- **Decides:** where one of Q1's matches has a side that lands in a later part, that later part writes the join. The earlier side stays single, since a part's checker sees only its own landings.
- **Why:** an earlier part can't cite what isn't landed yet ([method-report.md L196](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L196)).
- **Risks:** if the later part missed the match, it would stay single, and two capabilities would hold one thing. The merge's one-capability questions would show it.
- **Instead:** let the coordinator write such joins at the merge.
- **Weight:** low. It decides who writes a join, not whether it is made.
- **Source:** [method-report.md L196](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L196)

#### A capability's parent follows the platforms' own grouping
- **Decides:** a capability sits under a parent where the platforms' own grouping puts it there, as a statement shows. Where their groupings differ, the one they share is followed. Failing that, Shopify's and Salla's admin grouping is followed, and the other goes in the note. Transfers and purchase orders were moved under new parents by it.
- **Why:** the dry run's review found parents set by the part's own sense, not the platforms' ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L24](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L24)).
- **Risks:** a parent moves no count or kind. It moves where a capability shows on the Knowledge Map's page, and which coarser thing a nested ruling points to.
- **Instead:** a tree of the model's own, by function, the same for every platform.
- **Weight:** low. Only the tree's shape would move.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L146](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L146)

#### Names use every platform's own terms
- **Decides:** a capability's name uses every landed platform's terms, and avoids a word that means something else on one of them. A word that means two things goes in the capability's note. A mapping part proposes a rename; the coordinator renames at the merge.
- **Why:** you read a capability in each platform's own words, and one word can name different things on two platforms ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L31](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L31); [review-dry-run-rulings.md L45](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L45)).
- **Risks:** a misleading name, nothing counted.
- **Instead:** a neutral name of the model's own, with each platform's terms only in the note.
- **Weight:** low. Wording only.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L148](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L148)

#### A concern cited in another tree is marked strained
- **Decides:** a landed concern whose statements are cited in another tree of capabilities is marked strained, and its reading says where else it is cited, so the reviewer can split it.
- **Why:** such a concern holds more than one thing ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L23](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L23)).
- **Risks:** 219 of the 652 landings are strained (`merged/landings.tsv`). The mark moves nothing itself. It sends each one to the review.
- **Instead:** split every such concern at once, before the review. That is more parts, on readings the review may undo.
- **Weight:** low. It marks; it moves nothing.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L147](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L147)

### 3. Scope and conditions

#### A kind carries its scope
- **Decides:** a capability that holds only under a plan, a beta or one observed store says so in its kind. It is a class with that scope, not a class in general.
- **Why:** a platform that offers a thing only on its top plan, or in one country, doesn't offer it to every merchant ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L49](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L49)).
- **Risks:** five classes hold only under a scope (`merged/kinds.tsv`): CM-MKT-004 (Plus), CM-MKT-021 (Shopify's plans and one country's site), CM-SHP-024 and CM-STF-007 (one Zid plan each), and CM-TRU-019 (a list of countries). If a scope were read wrongly, a class would read as general when it isn't, or the other way round. No count moves.
- **Instead:** count every class alike, and leave scope to the cells.
- **Weight:** medium. It moves scope, not counts.
- **Source:** [review-dry-run-rulings.md L49](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L49)

#### Who fills a slot comes only from a statement
- **Decides:** a slot's filler is a third party or a law that a statement names as doing the capability or setting its terms. A flag's name isn't one. Its market comes only from the statement's market scope.
- **Why:** fillers are each market's setup, which the subjects need, and a name in code says nothing of who does the work ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L47](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L47)).
- **Risks:** 2,476 filler rows (`merged/slot-fillers.tsv`). Fillers don't enter counts, but they are what a subject reads for a market. A filler read from a flag would name a provider the platform may never have offered.
- **Instead:** take fillers from every name, flags included, marked as names only.
- **Weight:** medium. It moves what the markets read, not counts.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L150](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L150)

### 4. What is no thing of the class

No ruling before the held pass decides that something is no thing of the class. The `out` rulings are the held pass's, in Part 1.

### 5. What the model reads, and how

#### Nothing held never counts as lacking, and an unplaced capability stays open
- **Decides:** a cell with nothing held means only that the concern map holds nothing found by the terms searched. It never means the platform lacks the capability, and it never lowers a count. A capability that no platform places is reported as open, with the gap that would place it. It is never guessed into a class or a slot. Listed first on the orientation page, item 4.
- **Why:** the concern map holds what was captured, not everything a platform does ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L50](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L50)).
- **Risks:** 620 of the 1,835 cells hold nothing (`merged/cells.tsv`). Read as absences, they would tell you 620 things the evidence doesn't say. Two capabilities are open and unplaced, and seven are slots (`merged/kinds.tsv`). If a guess had been allowed, those two would carry a kind. The concern map understated counts wherever the evidence sat outside the pages Q1 placed ([method-report.md L230](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L230)), so many of the 620 are likely gaps of capture (a reading).
- **Instead:** read nothing held, after a search, as the platform not having it, and give an unplaced capability its likeliest kind. Counts wouldn't move, since they count only what platforms place. But the page would state up to 620 absences, and the two open capabilities would carry a guessed kind.
- **Weight:** high. Two kinds, and what the page says of 620 cells, rest on it.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L111](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L111)

#### An inventory entry with no statement may be cited by its id
- **Decides:** where no statement covers it, a cell may cite an entry that Q1 placed in a concern, by its inventory id, such as SH-I-0902. Searches read the entries too, so a search before writing nothing held covers them.
- **Why:** the dry run's review found entries placed without statements, which no search could see. Writing new statements into the concern map, which is frozen, was the other way ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L17](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L17)).
- **Risks:** 897 evidence rows in 398 cells cite an inventory id. 14 cells count on inventory ids alone (Zid 5, BigCommerce 5, WooCommerce 3, Shopify 1), and one capability's kind rests on them: CM-B2B-005. An entry is a name in a list, so if a name isn't enough, those 14 places would go, and that kind with them.
- **Instead:** cite only statements, and leave an entry with no statement as a gap.
- **Weight:** high. Fourteen counted places and one kind rest on it.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L144](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L144)

#### The model cites the subjects' own statements, in the coordinator's amendments only
- **Decides:** a subject's statement, by its id (such as S01-SA-104), may be cited only in the coordinator's amendments from the subjects' points, where a conflict showed the subject holds what the concern map doesn't. A part cites the concern map's keys.
- **Why:** the subjects read runs, released code and help pages that Q1 never placed. Leaving them out would keep counts the evidence already corrects ([method-report.md L220](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L220)).
- **Risks:** 109 evidence rows in 37 cells of 27 capabilities cite a subject's statement (`merged/evidence.tsv`). The method report counts 21 capabilities that changed kind or count, one of them from open to class ([method-report.md L224](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L224)). Without the ruling, they would go back.
- **Instead:** keep the model to the concern map, and capture the subjects' sources into it first.
- **Weight:** high. 21 capabilities' kind or count rest on it.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L153](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L153)

#### Arabic is searched as it is written
- **Decides:** the search reads ة as ه and ى as ي, and ignores case, diacritics, hamza seats and tatweel. Each Arabic term is searched in its singular and its plural, with and without ال.
- **Why:** a broken plural is another word, not a longer one, and ة against ه missed matches ([method-report.md L20](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L20)).
- **Risks:** a search that misses would write nothing held for a cell the statements fill. Salla's and Zid's nothing-held cells rest on these searches. If the rule still misses a form, some of them are false.
- **Instead:** search by root, which finds more, and more noise.
- **Weight:** medium. It moves cells between held and nothing held. A count moves only where a missed statement places the cell.
- **Source:** [method-report.md L20](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L20)

#### Another domain's statement may support a placement, and the merge watches it
- **Decides:** a concern that names this domain among its other domains may support a placement here. The merge flags a statement that supports built in or own service in two domains, so nothing counts twice unseen.
- **Why:** Q1's concerns cross domains, and a thing's evidence can sit under another domain's concern ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L46](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L46)).
- **Risks:** without it, cells would miss evidence held next door. With it, a statement may count in two domains, which the rulings on counting once settle.
- **Instead:** a domain cites only its own concerns, and gaps are left where the evidence sits elsewhere.
- **Weight:** medium. Cells' support moves, and counts only through the rulings above.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L149](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L149)

#### A statement's words as a whole decide, the description over the title
- **Decides:** a statement's words as a whole decide what it shows. Where its description narrows its title, the description governs, the same way for every platform in a join.
- **Why:** a title can be broader than what the page says ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L43](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L43)).
- **Risks:** a cell placed on a broad title would claim more than the statement does. Not counted here.
- **Instead:** read the title alone, which is what most app-store categories hold.
- **Weight:** medium. Cells' support moves.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L145](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L145)

#### A capability the uncited statements show is added
- **Decides:** where statements no cell cites show a capability the model lacks, it is added, as pricing was, if its statements bear it out. The other uncited statements are cited where they bear on a placement.
- **Why:** the dry run's structure review found statements left uncited that show a thing the model didn't hold ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L30](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L30)).
- **Risks:** a capability added from leftovers could duplicate one another domain holds, which the one-domain search guards against.
- **Instead:** leave uncited statements to Q3's check for misses.
- **Weight:** medium. It adds cells, and any count it adds is a new capability's.
- **Source:** [review-dry-run-rulings.md L30](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L30)

#### Architecture reads each capability's data and events, exact where cited, coarse otherwise
- **Decides:** the merge derives each capability's data, events and deciding statements in two grades. The statements its cells cite are attributed exactly. The other statements of the concerns landed in it are attributed by landing, coarsely, and each area's design refines them. Parts write nothing more. Listed first on the orientation page, item 6.
- **Why:** it keeps the parts' cost where it was, and says plainly what is coarse ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L48](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L48)).
- **Risks:** of 14,236 rows in architecture's index, 12,480 are exact and 1,756 are by landing (`merged/interfaces.tsv`). A row by landing may name a field or an event that belongs to another capability of the same concern, until the area's design checks it.
- **Instead:** have each part attribute every data field and event to a capability. That is more cost per part, and no coarse rows.
- **Weight:** medium. Architecture's inputs move, not the model's counts.
- **Source:** [review-dry-run-rulings.md L48](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L48)

#### A statement's citation is part of the fact
- **Decides:** where a statement is a bare line, such as an access-scope requirement, its citation's source and locator say what it is about. A requirement line found on the page of Shopify's URL-redirect mutation shows that the mutation exists. Listed first on the orientation page, item 12.
- **Why:** the citation is traced to its source at a commit, so it is a fact, not a note's inference ([E-backfill-zid/notes.md L40](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/E-backfill-zid/notes.md?plain=1#L40)).
- **Risks:** it placed Shopify's cell in URL redirects, CM-CNT-009, as built in. That cell now counts on 11 rows, the held pass's among them, so its count of 3 no longer rests on this ruling alone.
- **Instead:** read only a statement's own text, and leave such a line as shows.
- **Weight:** medium. It moved one cell's support, and that cell's count now holds without it.
- **Source:** [E-backfill-zid/notes.md L40](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/parts/E-backfill-zid/notes.md?plain=1#L40)

#### Q3's check counts a question covered when a capability holds its thing
- **Decides:** a Knowledge Map question or payment row is covered when every thing it asks about has a capability with its cells. Detail it asks beyond the cells (states, fields, rules, a flow's steps) waits for the model's detail at that area after GM. A covered row is covered whatever the placements, since the cells say who holds what. A question that one of your decisions in force already answers is beyond the class, marked as yours, decided. A row never adds a capability itself: where it shows something missing, the coordinator decides from the evidence. Listed first on the orientation page, item 20. No rule in the brief is marked as a ruling, so these are its rules as the page and the method report state them.
- **Why:** the extent holds capabilities and who places them, not their detail. The inspection found that covered had no grain ([Q3-check.md L21](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/Q3-check.md?plain=1#L21); [method-report.md L183](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L183)).
- **Risks:** of the 260 rows, 163 are covered, 34 partly and 63 beyond the class. 175 rows name detail for after GM, and 39 are answered as your decision (`q3/check-*.tsv`). If covered needed the detail too, many of the 163 would be partly, and their rest would be misses to amend before GM.
- **Instead:** count a row covered only when the cells answer it whole, detail included.
- **Weight:** medium. It moves what counts as a miss, not the model's counts.
- **Source:** [Q3-check.md L21](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/Q3-check.md?plain=1#L21)

#### The independent review reads by risk, not every row
- **Decides:** the final review reads, chosen by script: every cell a kind or a count hinges on, every join on role, every change the coordinator made, every question the merge leaves, the subjects' conflicts, Q3's misses, and a seeded sample of every part. Listed first on the orientation page, item 21.
- **Why:** the dry run's cells review read every row and cost almost twice the part it reviewed. The conventions and the checker now carry what that review found ([method-report.md L58](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L58); [method-report.md L185](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L185)).
- **Risks:** an error in a row the selection leaves out, and the sample misses, stays. The review hasn't run at this commit (no `studies/class-model/review/` folder), so its size isn't counted here. The method report estimated it at about 1,100 items ([method-report.md L186](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L186)).
- **Instead:** review every row. In the dry run's measure, that is the most expensive part of all.
- **Weight:** medium. It moves what errors can be found, not a count itself.
- **Source:** [R-extent.md L5](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/R-extent.md?plain=1#L5)

#### Kinds that rest on one thing are listed with the report
- **Decides:** each kind that rests on another domain's statements, on one convention or on one gap is listed with the extent's report.
- **Why:** those are the kinds most likely to move, and you see them before the model is used ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L32](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L32)).
- **Risks:** a fragile kind not listed would pass without your eye on it.
- **Instead:** leave fragility to the review's own findings.
- **Weight:** low. It decides what the report shows.
- **Source:** [review-dry-run-rulings.md L32](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L32)

#### A Q1 gap that limits a cell is named in its reading
- **Decides:** where a gap Q1 recorded on a cell's concern changes what the cell can claim, the reading says so. For a capability its platform names but never defines, the other platforms' nothing-held cells say the definition isn't stated.
- **Why:** a cell should claim no more than its evidence ([review-dry-run-rulings.md L34](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/review-dry-run-rulings.md?plain=1#L34)).
- **Risks:** wording; a reader could take a cell for more than it holds.
- **Instead:** keep gaps in Q1's files only.
- **Weight:** low. Wording only.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L151](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L151)

#### What counts as a conflict when a subject's point is placed
- **Decides:** a conflict about a count and one about a placement follow the model's own rules, and don't overlap. A cell that already places the third party is no conflict. A page that is a whole corpus gives no leads, and the subjects' copies of help pages trace to the concern map as it cites them.
- **Why:** the brief's two inspections found conflicts raised on cells that already held the answer ([method-report.md L202](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L202); [method-report.md L206](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L206)).
- **Risks:** a real conflict read as none would leave a cell as it was. The 61 points that landed with no conflict rest on it ([method-report.md L218](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L218)).
- **Instead:** raise every difference between a point and a cell, and let the coordinator sort them.
- **Weight:** low. It decides what is raised, and the review reads a seeded tenth of the points with no conflict ([R-extent.md L35](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/R-extent.md?plain=1#L35)).
- **Source:** [method-report.md L202](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L202)

### 6. How the work is run

#### Engineering settles a subject's conflict first, and brings you only those under your decisions
- **Decides:** when the subjects' points are placed on the model, the drafting agent only records each conflict. The coordinator settles the fact first. A conflict comes to you only where it questions the evidence under one of your decisions, such as Decision 1, and then with its evidence, options and a recommendation. A conflict the evidence can't settle is kept open, with what would settle it. The brief was inspected twice before it ran ([method-report.md L200](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L200)). Listed first on the orientation page, item 19.
- **Why:** facts are engineering's to settle from the evidence, and your decisions are yours ([method-report.md L201](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L201)).
- **Risks:** none of the 50 outcomes came to you ([method-report.md L219](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L219)). If the line between a fact and your decision were drawn wrongly, a conflict you should have seen was settled without you. The table in section 1 lets you check each.
- **Instead:** bring you every conflict that touches a row of your decisions, settled or not.
- **Weight:** medium. It moves who sees an outcome, not the outcome.
- **Source:** [method-report.md L201](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L201)

#### Parts run at high effort, not maximum
- **Decides:** the parts run at high effort, not maximum, now that the conventions are settled and the checker carries the dry run's tests. A part whose check or review shows gaps is run again. An agent launched without its setting, which inherited maximum, was stopped and launched again at high. Listed first on the orientation page, item 7.
- **Why:** cost. Anchors at high cost about 0.75 million tokens a concern, against the dry run's 1.5 ([method-report.md L117](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L117)). The conventions and the checker's tests carry what maximum effort was buying ([method-report.md L104](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L104)).
- **Risks:** a part at high effort may read less closely. The final review's sample is where that would show.
- **Instead:** maximum for every part, at about twice the cost (a reading of those rates).
- **Weight:** low. The work's cost.
- **Source:** [method-report.md L104](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L104)

#### A large part runs in batches, each a fresh agent
- **Decides:** a part is cut small enough that its agent's context stays under about 200,000 tokens. A large part runs as a series of batches, each by a fresh agent that continues from the files. Listed first on the orientation page, item 7.
- **Why:** a part's cost grows with the square of its length, since every request re-reads the whole context. The anchor's last quarter cost five times its first ([method-report.md L38](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L38); [method-report.md L75](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L75)).
- **Risks:** a batch sees the earlier batches only through the files, so a reading one batch made may go stale in a later one. The coordinator's corrections between batches caught some thirty ([method-report.md L162](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L162)).
- **Instead:** one agent per part, whatever its length, at close to twice the cost.
- **Weight:** low. The work's cost.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L101](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L101)

#### A script's candidate statements are leads, never evidence
- **Decides:** a mapping part judges a short list of each capability's candidate statements on its platform, found by the terms the other platforms' cells use. The candidates are leads, read with their keys, never evidence, and the part searches further where they don't settle a cell.
- **Why:** most of a mapping part's cost was its cells, designing a search for each ([method-report.md L47](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L47)).
- **Risks:** a cell judged only on its leads could miss a statement in other words. A miss would be nothing held where the statements hold something.
- **Instead:** a search designed for every cell. In the dry run that cost 0.46 million tokens a cell, against 0.18 for the next part with the script ([method-report.md L97](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L97)).
- **Weight:** low. The work's cost, with a small risk to nothing-held cells.
- **Source:** [B-extent.md L95](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/B-extent.md?plain=1#L95)

#### An inspector runs the scripts, and an exact fix needs no third round
- **Decides:** every inspection of a brief that comes with scripts runs them, on fixtures it builds. A re-inspection follows a large rewrite. A fix the inspector specified exactly is applied, then tested on the inspector's own fixtures, without a third round.
- **Why:** an inspector that runs the scripts finds what reading the brief can't, and most new problems came from the fixes ([method-report.md L9](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L9); [method-report.md L10](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L10)).
- **Risks:** an exact fix that brings a new fault would go unseen until a run. The points brief's first inspection did bring one, N2 ([method-report.md L206](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L206)).
- **Instead:** a third round after every rewrite.
- **Weight:** low. The work's cost and checks.
- **Source:** [method-report.md L10](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L10)

#### Amendments run only between drafting rounds
- **Decides:** the coordinator amends the model only between the drafters' rounds, never while a drafter is reading it.
- **Why:** the first amendment ran while two drafters were reading the model, and both had to stop and resume ([method-report.md L229](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L229)).
- **Risks:** a slower round.
- **Instead:** amend at once, and let the drafters' warning stop them.
- **Weight:** low. The work's cost.
- **Source:** [method-report.md L229](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/method-report.md?plain=1#L229)


## From the held pass on

### 1. Who does it: built in, its own service, apps, outside, or shown only; and who makes it

#### The platform's own list of third parties' things is left to apps; a card that names no maker is built in, or shown only where it is a suggestion
- **Decides:** where the platform lists third parties' things to connect or install, such as a channel catalog, a list of tax services or a section for accounting software, each card that names a third party's product is left to apps, the third party its filler. A card that names no one, or only the venue (a marketplace, an ad platform), is built in, the missing maker noted as a gap. A card that names a company of the platform's group is its own service. Refined after WC-3: the platform's suggestion to buy an extension from its marketplace, naming no maker (WooCommerce's `woocommerce_marketplace_suggestions`), is shown only. Built in is kept for a card of the platform's own catalog of connections (BigCommerce's channels). Applies on every platform. (M-3, M-7; refined after WC-3)
- **Why:** B-extent's left to apps is "something the platform provides" ([rulings, line 8](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L8)), and the platform's own list is that. A suggestion naming no maker shows the extension exists, as the model already places WooCommerce Subscriptions ([rulings, line 9](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L9)).
- **Risks:** 330 lines name M-3 and 204 name M-7, most of them WooCommerce's (219 and 160), and 9 name the refinement. If a card naming no maker is a third party's thing, those built-in lines count toward the class where they should be a slot. That moves counts where a cell rests on such cards (a reading).
- **Instead:** a card with no maker named is shown only, since nothing shows who does it. That lowers counts wherever such a card is a cell's only support.
- **Weight:** high: over 500 lines, and the counts of the cells that rest on cards.
- **Source:** [rulings, lines 8 to 9](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L8-L9)

#### A first-party app's own features are its own service, wherever held words name the platform as its maker
- **Decides:** an app of the platform's own, installed from its App Store, at an `apps/<handle>` route or called 'the X app', is its own service on each line about its own features. That holds wherever held words name the platform as its maker: 'we’ve added … to X', 'our … in X', 'first-party', 'from Shopify', or a store listing's developer. Its plan lines stay built in, as does the admin's page for it. Third parties' extensions of it stay left to apps, and the online store and POS stay built in. (the ruling after SH-55)
- **Why:** B-extent's own service is a separate product of the platform's company. The changelog's 'we' statements name Shopify as maker ([rulings, lines 128 to 131](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L128-L131)).
- **Risks:** 396 lines name it. Aligned under it: Shopify Flow's 38 lines, Shopify Collective's 17, Shopify Email's 5 and Translate & Adapt's 1 ([rulings, lines 133 to 137](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L133-L137)). Built in and its own service both count, so a wrong call rarely moves a count. It would show Shopify's apps as a separate service where they are part of the platform (a reading).
- **Instead:** keep an app built in unless a contract names a separate company, as Zid Pay's terms do. Few lines would move, and the class would read Shopify's apps as part of its core.
- **Weight:** high: 396 lines.
- **Source:** [rulings, lines 128 to 139](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L128-L139)

#### An app or separate product with no maker statement held is shown only, whatever its name
- **Decides:** where no held statement names a maker, the app's feature is shown only, the maker a gap. The platform's possessive ('Shopify's Geolocation app') counts as a maker statement. The admin's page for the app, the admin's own route or section for it, and a plan line that includes it stay built in. It is left to apps only where the statement shows a third party making it. This replaced the earlier 'built in by what the post shows, the maker a gap' (the second part of the ruling after SH-36). (the ruling after SH-43)
- **Why:** B-extent's shown only is a statement that shows the platform has the capability and "supports no placement" ([rulings, line 159](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L159)). A brand in the name shows neither built in nor left to apps.
- **Risks:** 89 lines name it. The alignment changed 48 lines more, among them 26 from built in to shown only and 8 from left to apps to shown only ([align-no-maker.md, line 162](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/shopify/align-no-maker.md?plain=1#L162)). It moves a count wherever such a line was a cell's only built-in support: Shopify's CM-ONB-004 kept its built in only through one other line ([align-no-maker.md, line 128](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/shopify/align-no-maker.md?plain=1#L128)). If Shopify's branded apps are its own, these cells undercount Shopify.
- **Instead:** read Shopify's brand on an app as its own service, or keep the app built in. Both would count Shopify on those cells.
- **Weight:** high: it moves cells between counted and not counted.
- **Source:** [rulings, lines 159 to 163](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L159-L163)

#### A page at an app's address, with no maker or gate stated, is shown only on what the app does, and built in as the admin's page for an app
- **Decides:** a page the platform's navigation lists under its brand at an app's address, such as Zid Ads or Zid Voice, is shown only on what the app does, the missing maker a gap. It is built in on CM-APP-007, the dashboard's page for an app. (M-10)
- **Why:** the page shows no third party, and no maker ([rulings, line 12](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L12)).
- **Risks:** 104 lines name it, 101 of them Shopify's. If such apps are the platform's own, their capabilities undercount the platform.
- **Instead:** built in on what the app does, as the platform's own admin place. That raises the count wherever the app is the cell's only support.
- **Weight:** high: 104 lines, and counts.
- **Source:** [rulings, line 12](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L12)

#### What a partner or an app asks of a merchant is shown only; only the staff permission is built in
- **Decides:** a collaborator request, a Partner API permission set, an app's install consent, or Salla's partner access requests are shown only. Only the staff permission is built in. (the brief's ruling, from the dry run's S7 as `inspection-H.md`'s B-1 widened it)
- **Why:** they state what may be asked for, not what the platform does ([H-held.md, line 117](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/H-held.md?plain=1#L117)). A reading: the brief gives no further reason.
- **Risks:** 235 lines name S7. If such a permission shows a capability the platform offers, those cells would hold more built-in lines.
- **Instead:** built in on the capability the permission gates, as staff permissions are. More cells would be held as built in.
- **Weight:** high: 235 lines.
- **Source:** [H-held.md, line 117](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/H-held.md?plain=1#L117)

#### A permission of a partners' portal takes the support of the page it gates
- **Decides:** a permission in a platform's partners' portal, which gates what a partner's own team does there, takes the support of the page it gates. A partner's tool for a marketplace of third parties, such as the expert marketplace, stays left to apps. The platform's own billing is built in. This narrowed the brief's earlier ruling, which made every portal permission built in on what it gates ([H-held.md, line 117](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/H-held.md?plain=1#L117)). (the ruling after SA-30, extending the ruling after SA-44)
- **Why:** the brief's 'each permission is built in' is for a store's staff permissions over the platform's own capabilities ([rulings, line 93](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L93)).
- **Risks:** 152 lines name it, 141 of them Salla's. If those partner tools are the platform's own, they undercount Salla.
- **Instead:** the brief's first wording: every portal permission built in on what it gates. Salla's marketplace tools would count as built in.
- **Weight:** high: 152 lines.
- **Source:** [rulings, line 93](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L93)

#### Maker evidence held outside the entry counts
- **Decides:** a held statement naming a company as of the platform's group counts wherever it is held: the entry, another source of the batch, or a cell's own maker evidence. The line cites it. So BigCommerce's Feedonomics cards are its own service, on the terms page's "any of Commerce's affiliates, subsidiaries, including, but not limited to Feedonomics Holdings, LLC" ([rulings, line 7](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L7)). (M-1)
- **Why:** B-extent's maker evidence is a statement naming the company. Nothing ties it to one entry ([rulings, line 7](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L7)).
- **Risks:** 95 lines name it, 45 of them WooCommerce's and 16 BigCommerce's. Its own service counts where left to apps doesn't, so it moves counts: four BigCommerce cells were edited from M-1 and M-2 (commit `0814b925`'s message).
- **Instead:** only the entry's own words name a maker. Feedonomics would be left to apps, and BigCommerce's count would drop on those capabilities.
- **Weight:** high: it moves counts.
- **Source:** [rulings, line 7](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L7)

#### A maker field counts only where merchants are shown it
- **Decides:** a field the platform's own code shows merchants as a maker statement names the maker. WooCommerce's `is_built_by_wc: true`, shown as 'Built by WooCommerce', makes its own service. A field no admin asset shows, such as the payments suggestions' `made_in_woo`, names no maker, the more so where merchants read another maker: 'PayPal Payments by PayPal'. (the ruling after WC-4; the brief's ruling)
- **Why:** the release defines `made_in_woo` only in a code comment, and no admin asset shows it ([rulings, line 47](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L47)).
- **Risks:** 287 lines' readings name one field or the other. Its own service counts and left to apps doesn't, so a wrong reading of the tag moves WooCommerce's counts on its payment capabilities (a reading).
- **Instead:** read `made_in_woo` as WooCommerce's maker statement. The tagged payment extensions would be its own service, and counted.
- **Weight:** high: it moves counts on WooCommerce's payments.
- **Source:** [rulings, line 47](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L47); [H-held.md, line 110](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/H-held.md?plain=1#L110)

#### Salla's App Store `company` field is a maker statement
- **Decides:** the App Store shows each app's company to merchants, so an app whose company is سلة (Salla's own company, id 2038173539) is Salla's. 83 apps carry it. One that does the thing itself, as the Blog app does the store's blog, is its own service on that thing's capability. One that connects a third party, as Zapier does, is left to apps, the third party its filler. Together they are the lead for CM-APP-011, the platform's own apps, which holds nothing on Salla. (the ruling after SA-17)
- **Why:** merchants are shown the field on the app's card and on a page per company, unlike WooCommerce's unshown tag ([rulings, line 94](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L94)).
- **Risks:** 18 lines name it. It decides which of 83 apps count as Salla's own service. If سلة is only the publisher of others' integrations, those cells overcount Salla.
- **Instead:** read the field as the publisher only, and place each app by its listing's words. Fewer of Salla's cells would be its own service.
- **Weight:** high: it moves counts for Salla's apps.
- **Source:** [rulings, lines 94 to 97](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L94-L97)

#### A list of providers the platform's own words call its integrations is built in
- **Decides:** where the platform calls a list of providers its integrations, such as BigCommerce's 'BigCommerce gateway integrations' and the gateways it 'supports', the list is built in, each provider its filler. (M-4)
- **Why:** the platform's own words say it connects them, and each row links the platform's own 'Connecting with' article ([rulings, line 10](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L10)).
- **Risks:** 35 lines name it, 22 of them BigCommerce's. Built in counts and left to apps doesn't. So if gateways are third parties doing the work through the platform, BigCommerce's payment cells overcount, where they rest on these tables (a reading).
- **Instead:** left to apps, the provider doing the work through the platform's gateway interface. BigCommerce's count could drop on its gateway capabilities.
- **Weight:** high, a reading: it can move BigCommerce's count.
- **Source:** [rulings, line 10](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L10)

#### Once a new thing is a capability, each line's support is what its entry shows
- **Decides:** lines placed as shown only just because the thing had no place of its own become built in where the platform's own words say it does the thing (SH-36's 'search has been improved'). Tinker's and Planet's its own service stand on their maker statements. Finance's lines go to the capabilities of the pages they name, or are dropped with the group. (the coordinator's decision on the proposal)
- **Why:** the brief writes a new thing with no place of its own as shown only, a rule for a thing not yet decided ([new-proposals.md, line 135](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/new-proposals.md?plain=1#L135)).
- **Risks:** it moves the new capabilities' cells from shown only to counted (a reading: the amendment parts applied it, and their counts aren't separated here).
- **Instead:** keep shown only until the second stage reads each.
- **Weight:** high: it sets the new capabilities' counts.
- **Source:** [new-proposals.md, line 135](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/new-proposals.md?plain=1#L135); [amendment notes, line 36](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/shopify/amendment-notes.md?plain=1#L36)

#### The platform's possessive, its 'we' and its channels: the corrections after SH-45
Shared risk: each correction moves Shopify's lines between left to apps and its own service, which changes counts, or between built in and its own service, which doesn't. Shared alternative: read Shopify's brand and possessive as naming no maker, and leave these lines as the batches placed them.

| Ruling | Decides | Why | Weight | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The Google app is Shopify's own | The Google & YouTube app (formerly the Google channel and the Google Shopping app) is Shopify's own app. Its own features are its own service, Google or YouTube the venue: selling there, syncing products there, the campaigns it creates. Its admin page is built in. Google's own services through it (Google Analytics, YouTube's affiliate programme) stay left to apps. It overturned the check after SH-42, which had the channel left to apps. | Held posts say 'Shopify's Google channel' and 'Shopify's Google Shopping app'; the possessive names the provider (the ruling after SH-43) | medium: 10 lines aligned; 19 lines name the corrections | [rulings, lines 165 to 167](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L165-L167) |
| The Buy Button channel's own features | They are its own service, on four held posts' 'we've made the following updates'. The pricing rows stay built in, as plan lines. | the 'we' cue | medium | [rulings, line 168](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L168) |
| A possessive on a part of the platform | 'Shopify's fastest way to check out' keeps Shop Pay in the store's checkout built in. The possessive names a separate product's provider only where the thing is an app, device or service of its own. | Shop Pay in checkout is the platform's own checkout | medium | [rulings, line 169](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L169) |

#### Shopify's changelog and Editions speak as the platform
- **Decides:** a changelog or Editions post where 'we' make, upgrade or ship an app is a maker statement. The app's own things are its own service, as 'a new app from Shopify' is for Audiences. A post that only names an app is no maker statement. Shop's things stay its own service. (the rulings after SH-35 and SH-53)
- **Why:** the changelog and Editions are Shopify's own words about itself ([rulings, line 111](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L111); [rulings, line 119](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L119)).
- **Risks:** 56 lines name the ruling after SH-35, and 37 the rulings after SH-53, about 24 of them on the free themes (below). Its own service and built in both count, so few counts move. If 'we' is the platform speaking of its core, the class shows separate services where there is one platform (a reading).
- **Instead:** a maker statement only where a separate company or product is named. Those apps' lines would be built in.
- **Weight:** medium.
- **Source:** [rulings, line 111](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L111); [rulings, line 119](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L119)

#### A maker named in any held post makes every line about the app its own service, renames included
- **Decides:** where the platform's own words name an app's maker in any held post, every line about the app is its own service, under its old names too. Shopify Messaging, formerly Shopify Email, is the case: a post states the rename, and SH-34's, SH-35's and SH-36's nine lines on it were aligned. (the ruling after SH-37, the first; with M-1)
- **Why:** M-1 makes maker evidence count wherever it is held; a rename is the same app ([rulings, line 121](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L121)).
- **Risks:** about 82 lines name the ruling after SH-37 with a maker or a rename. If a renamed app is a different product, its old lines take a maker that isn't theirs.
- **Instead:** each line by its own entry's words, as before M-1.
- **Weight:** medium.
- **Source:** [rulings, line 121](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L121)

#### The platform's own free themes are built in, the store's own storefront
- **Decides:** Shopify's free themes are built in, the store's own storefront, as Salla's 'any Salla-developed theme' is. 'Our free themes' makes no separate service. It overturned SH-53's E21. (the ruling after SH-53, the second)
- **Why:** the theme is the storefront the platform gives every store ([rulings, line 120](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L120)).
- **Risks:** about 24 lines name the ruling with themes. Built in and its own service both count, so no count moves.
- **Instead:** E21's reading: its own service, by 'our'.
- **Weight:** low.
- **Source:** [rulings, line 120](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L120)

#### A separate product or service of the platform's is its own service, as an app is
- **Decides:** the ruling after SH-55 holds for any separate product or service of the platform's, not only apps. Shopify Credit is a card Shopify says it built ('That’s why we built Shopify Credit'), so its own features are its own service. The admin's Credit routes and the plan rows stay built in, and the card's issuers outside. A changelog post filed under Apps that names Shopify as its feature's maker makes that app the platform's own: Fraud Control's checkout rules are its own service. It overturned SH-41's own rule 3. (the ruling after SH-41)
- **Why:** B-extent's own service is the maker or provider of "a separate product, app, extension or service" ([H-held.md, line 110](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/briefs/H-held.md?plain=1#L110)).
- **Risks:** 34 lines name it, and 7 were aligned. Its own service and built in both count, so it moves wording more than counts.
- **Instead:** keep money products in the admin built in. Credit's lines would read built in.
- **Weight:** medium.
- **Source:** [rulings, line 141](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L141)

#### The corrections with the Editions digest, and after SH-41
Shared risk: each correction sets one product's support across many lines. A wrong one misplaces that product on every line about it. Shared alternative: leave each line as its batch placed it, for the final review to settle.

| Correction | Decides | Why | Weight | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The Fulfillment Network is Shopify's own | its own service on its own features; its partners' work left to apps | maker statements held: 'our network of 20+ distribution hubs and fulfillment centers' | medium | [rulings, line 143](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L143) |
| Shopify Balance is built in | the admin's own money account, as its cell reads it; a 'we've added … using Shopify Balance' post doesn't make it a separate product, as it doesn't for Shopify Payments | the cell reads it so | medium | [rulings, line 144](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L144) |
| An API naming an unnamed outside system is built in | left to apps only where its words say apps or partners do the work | B-extent | medium | [rulings, line 145](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L145) |
| A line on CM-MKS-003 (Managed Markets itself) takes no add-on scope | the offer itself is not scoped by its own name | the ruling after SH-70 | low | [rulings, line 146](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L146) |
| AI for merchants outside the admin goes on the place's capability | not CM-AIA-001, the platform's AI in the admin | CM-AIA-001's thing | medium | [rulings, line 147](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L147) |
| Managed Markets is built in | the admin's own service, Global-e outside where named; 'Our Merchant of Record capability' doesn't make a separate product. The Editions' 13 lines aligned | the test set for Shopify Balance | medium | [rulings, line 152](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L152) |
| POS Terminal is its own service | every line, on 'POS Terminal, Shopify’s newest, most powerful payment device'. 4 lines aligned | the maker statement | medium | [rulings, line 153](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L153) |
| The Combined Listings app is its own service | 2 lines aligned; its plan rows stay built in | 'From a new app for merchandising products' | medium | [rulings, line 154](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L154) |
| Unit pricing is CM-CAT-001 | a price kept on the product record | the ruling after SA-25 | low | [rulings, line 155](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L155) |
| A country's address fields | CM-CUS-008, with CM-CHK-001 where at checkout; validation and autocompletion CM-CUS-009 | the cells' readings | low | [rulings, line 156](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L156) |
| A sale credited to a staff member | takes `new:Staff sales attribution` (now CM-STF-006) beside CM-POS-001 | a thing no capability held | low | [rulings, line 157](https://github.com/Hamza-ye/sharrwah-knowledge-map/blob/27ba96d2024dd0b7bbb377a23d79351f5de8c363/studies/class-model/held/review-H-rulings.md?plain=1#L157) |
